DESCRIPTION:The exhilarating 2008 Cape Odyssey\, the first full service tra
 il running stage race in Africa and one of only a few similar events world 
 wide. The race\, in its second year\, will take place from 8 to 12 October 
 2008 covering 200km over 5 days. Runners will start in Hermanus and finish 
 in the university town of Stellenbosch in the Cape Winelands.\n\nRunners co
 mpete in teams of two and must stay together at all times or risk a time pe
 nalty. Teams can register in four different categories including Ladies\, M
 en\, Mixed and Masters (both runners must be 40 years +). The minimum age o
 f participation is 18 on the day the race starts.\n\nAt each stage\, the wi
 nners of the day will receive prizes and the leaders in the overall classif
 ication\, their Leader Vests.  After the last stage\, individual times will
  be calculated to reveal the overall winners.\n\nThe Cape Odyssey is brough
 t to you by the organisers of the world’s premier mountain bike stage race 
 and the most globally televised mountain bike event of all time\, the Absa 
 Cape Epic presented by adidas. The organisers aim to deliver an unforgettab
 le running and African travel-experience. Runners can focus on covering the
  daily distance and the experienced event crew will take care of everything
  else\, including tented accommodation in race villages\, water points en r
 oute\, and medical assistance.

DESCRIPTION:First Fridays’ in Hermanus is our part of a worldwide initiativ
 e to make art and art galleries more accessible to the public. On the first
  Friday of every month\, the thirteen art galleries in the center of Herman
 us remain open until 8pm. Pick up a map at any gallery\, stroll through the
  village\, savor a glass of wine from one of our local vineyards and enjoy 
 the astonishing range of art and fine craft on display.\n\nThe array of art
 works available at the participating galleries ensures something for every 
 palate. If you’re looking to spend R50 000 on an investment piece by a famo
 us South African master\, you’ll find it. If you’re searching for a delicat
 e little porcelain bowl at R100\, it’ll be waiting for you. If you want to 
 see an artist at work\, several of the galleries are also the home studios 
 of resident painters and sculptors whose completed works are displayed. Cer
 amics\, bronzes\, glassware\, jewelry\, watercolors\, African artifacts\; m
 ore than enough to fill an hour or two with delight and indulgence.\n\n13 G
 alleries – all within walking distance\n200 Artists\n3000 paintings\n150 sc
 ulptures and ceramics\n\nFor more info please feel free to get in contact v

DESCRIPTION:The SA IPT is considered the 2nd most important amateur golf to
 urnament in SA and will attract teams from all parts of the country and the
  full SA Golf Union Executive will be in attendance. Throughout the week\, 
 a media team will be documenting the competition to broadcast a 30 minute t
 elevised production afterwards.\n\nThe SA IPT was first played in 1960 and 
 hosted at Hermanus Golf Club in 1966 and 1973. It is amateur golf’s premier
  inter provincial team competition where 12 provinces are divided\, by stre
 ngth of playing ability\, into two Sections\, an A and B Section (6 teams i
 n each). There are 8 players to a team and each team plays one match agains
 t the others in the same Section. The winning team of the A Section is rega
 rded as the leading province in the country\, in much the same way as the C
 urrie Cup competition does for rugby.\n\nThe event attracts the top amateur
  players from across the country. The defending champions are Boland\, who 
 are also the host union in that Hermanus Golf Club is affiliated to it. Spe
 ctators are also usually in attendance of this event. The A Section compris
 es the defending champions\, Gauteng North\, Western Province\, Central Gau
 teng\, Ekurhuleni and Free State & Northern Cape. The B Section comprises K
 wazulu Natal\, Eastern Province\, Border\, Limpopo\, North West Province an
 d\nMpumalanga.\n\nThe event will generate a great deal of business for our 
 local community e.g. accommodation and hospitality\, renting of furniture\,
  food and beverage\, communication facilities and additional staff.\n\nWe a
 re indeed privileged that the SA Golf Association is hosting the event at H
 ermanus Golf Club and want to rally together to use this opportunity for th
 e promotion of Hermanus.\n\n\n\nTel: +27 28 3121954\nFax: +27 28 3122333\n\
 ne-mail: isabel@hgc.co.za \nwebsite: www.hgc.co.za

DESCRIPTION:The Hermanus History Society is presenting an illustrated talk 
 "Through the lens of Thoman Ravenscroft (1852-1948) - glimpses of our histo
 ry captured for posterity" at the auditorium from 17h30. \n\nThomas Ravensc
 roft was the official photographer of Cape Government Railways. A lot of hi
 s scenic photos\, mostly of the Boland and Western Cape\, decorated the tra
 in compartments.\n \nHermanus was his favourite place and he moved here ear
 ly in 1920. Next to his home "Linquenda"\, an old cottage in the Main Rd\, 
 where the post office now stands\, was a small wooden building which served
  as his studio. A sign on the building read "T D Ravenscroft ... photograph
 er for the last 65 years."\n \nFor more information on this great evening p
 lease contact Robin Lee on 028-3124072 or robin-lee@hermanus.co.za\n

 World but every year Hermanus displays its more artistic side with the Herm
 anus FynArts Festival. This year the festival will take place from the 06 –
  16 June 2014 and boasts a mass of visual arts and crafts\, a vast array of
  live musical performances\, food and wine events and much more. All of thi
 s makes for an unforgettable event for this small fisherman's town.\n\nAlon
 g with the static exhibitions\, old South African and Hollywood films are s
 creened in the Romantiques shop whilst galleries and several wine farms alo
 ng the Hemel en Aarde Wine Route (such as Hamilton Russell\, Creation\, La 
 Vierge and Ataraxia) host art exhibitions and other special events such as 
 painting classes and wine tastings. The musical section of the festival tak
 es place in the Anglican Church on a daily basis\, whilst tea parties\, win
 e and food tastings and virtual tours are on offer to anyone passing by. Fo
 r the more outdoorsy tourist\, there are guided tours of both our Fernkloof
  Nature Reserve and Cliff Path.\n\nAs the festival usually falls over the p
 ublic holiday of Youth Day an act or performance is done in remembrance of 
 this day and the sacrifices made. For the sake of the drinking public\, bus
  routes are set up on a “drop off and go” basis throughout town.\n\nThe fes
 tival is a stunning array of colours and sounds and experiences and the exc
 itement is palpable for this year's event.\n\nFor more information or to ob
 tain an itinerary of the event please contact the Hermanus Fynarts Office:\

 chool sports ground. Registration fee is R600.00 per team of ten and includ
 es ten luminaria bags.\n\nOne person can make a difference! In 1985\, one m
 an decided he had to do something to stop the endless pain\, suffering and 
 loss due to cancer. He decided to raise funds for his local American Cancer
  Society\, by doing something he enjoyed – running marathons. He spent an i
 ncredible 24 hours running around a track – a gruelling feat\, but represen
 tative of what someone fighting cancer endures. He asked for the support of
  people he knew and raised $27 000 USD.\n\nToday\, Relay For Life is a reco
 gnisable brand worldwide\, with 20 countries celebrating the lives saved fr
 om cancer\; remembering those lost and those fighting back… Relay For Life

 irst time this coming summer. Once the main set of shows ends in Cape Town\

 ies of world-class shows appearing at select venues between Cape Town and P
 lettenberg Bay.\n\nIGNSA’s summer series 2012 brings a focus onto those aco
 ustic guitar players who not only play the guitar purely instrumentally but
  who also use their skills to provide a scintillating and multi-textured ac
 companiment to their voices. This is in contrast to what is promulgated as 
 the norm\, whereby the song is sung with a bland strumming of the chords be
 hind the voice.\n\nCanadian Joel Fafard is a perfect example of this art an
 d brings his evocative\, thought provoking and hilarious show to Cape Town 
 for IGN’s summer series. Local acoustic guitar guru\, Tony Cox has toured e
 xtensively with Fafard in Canada and has this to say of the man…\n\n‘Joel i
 s one of the true gents of this world\, ridiculously talented\, just simply
  a great player\, great singer\, humble and so very down-to-earth…in his sh
 ows\, he gets all this across and delivers it all with a deadpan sense of h
 umour\, the combination is just killer!’\n\nAlso on the bill is another Can
 adian who is making a name for herself in South Africa and IGN’s first fema
 le guitarist\, Natasha Meister…along with the show’s creator\, South Africa
 n acoustic guitar guru\, Tony Cox.\n\nInternational Guitar Night is a serie
 s of performances held every summer and winter to bring to our South Africa
 n doorstep the world’s greatest and most accomplished acoustic guitar playe
 rs of the world today. IGN’s main focus is on those players who use their r
 ight-hand fingers to individually pluck the strings creating bass\, rhythm\
 , melody and percussive lines all at the same time\, like a mini-orchestra 
 coaxed out by the fingers of the right hand.

DESCRIPTION:"Whale Coast Conservation is bringing a whale of a festival to 
 Fisherman’s Village in Hermanus in celebration of Earth Hour. It will be a 
 day-long festival of education and entertainment that will lead visitors on
  a journey through education and trade into “Moonlight Avenue”\, and a uniq
 ue “Market by Moonlight”.  The highlight of the whole event will be the hou
 r between 20h30 and 21h30 of ‘un-plugged’ entertainment\, to unforgettably 
 mark Earth Hour 2014.\n   \nThe Market by Moonlight will be organised into 
 an ‘avenue’ of food and beverage vendors who will trade from stalls lit by 
 lanterns.  The ‘avenue’ will pass under trees\, also decorated and lit with
  lanterns to enhance the ambience.  The ‘avenue’ will lead to an entertainm
 ent courtyard featuring a full performance stage and continual program of i
 tems from mid-day onwards.\n\nThe aim is for visitors to browse\, taste and
  then eat while watching the wonderful entertainment.  \nThere is\, of cour
 se\, an environmental message. From 20h30 to 21h30 the lights will again be
  turned down as we symbolically show unity with at least 7\,000 cities and 
 towns in 150 countries who are all saying\, “I care about my planet!” \n\nT
 he special message of the evening will be the importance of wetlands in gen
 eral and\, in particular\, the great news about the planned restoration of 
 wetland at the grottoes at Grotto Beach. \nWhale Coast Conservation is grat
 eful to the National Lottery Distribution Trust Fund\, the Overstrand Munic
 ipality and Lawrence Heron’s Music and Theatre Management for their support
 . With this festival\, we aim to put Earth Hour firmly on the Hermanus tour
 ist and conservation maps.\n\nTo see more info about the Earth Hour School 
 Art competition go here\n \nPlease diarise the 29th of March at Fisherman’s
  Village in Hermanus and invite all your colleagues\, family and friends!"\
 n\nTaken from www.whalecoastconservation.org.za/events/45/30/Earth_Hour_201
 4\n\n

DESCRIPTION:The ringing of birds is a fascinating source of information. In
  addition to the close encounter when handling the bird\, the subsequent re
 capture or recovery of the bird can provide information on migration\, long
 evity\, mortality\, population studies\, territoriality\, feeding behaviour
  and other aspects that are studied by ornithologists.\n\nImagine the wealt
 h of information that can be gathered by one person over 15 years of bird-r
 inging. Mike Ford is such a person\, and he will show and tell at the Club’
 s monthly meeting on Wednesday evening 18 February.\n\nHe has a large pool 
 of photographs of birds in the hand and close-up\, and he will explain why 
 birds are ringed\, how they are trapped\, ringed\, measured and released\, 
 and how the data is archived and used.\n\nThe meeting is in the Fernkloof R
 eserve Hall and begins at seven. Tea\, coffee and biscuits in the kitchen a
 fterwards. Visitors are welcome.\n\nFor more information please contact the
  HBC on 083 227 9003

CREATED:20140303T094656Z
DESCRIPTION:Fernkloof Nature Reserve was declared a reserve in 1957 by the 
 Provincial Council of the Cape. To date about l600 species have been identi
 fied and are now housed in the Hermanus Herbarium at Fernkloof – more than 
 in the entire United Kingdom. The Herbarium has been accorded International
  Status and is continuously growing every year. In 1977 the building of the
  Botanical Centre began\, which comprised of a curator's office\, exhibitio
 n hall\, herbarium\, kitchen\, visitor's cottage and pathways out towards t
 he reserve. This was all funded and maintained by the Hermanus Botanical So
 ciety\, which still looks over the growth and management of Fernkloof Natur
 e Reserve. In season you can also find a seasonal wild flower exhibit in th
 e Visitor's Centre at the top of the car park.\n\nTo commemorate this beaut
 iful reserve the Annual Hermanus Wildflower Festival takes place at the res
 erve and displays a number of beautiful mini gardens\, floral and specimen 
 displays as well as many other indigenous plant life and fauna information.
  This year the event will take place from the 25 – 28 September 2014. Along
 side the beautiful displays and carefully arranged gardens\, the event host
 s an array of teas and luncheons for the crowds and opens it's shops for vi
 sitors to buy some of the plant life on display. \n\nFor more information p
 lease feel free to contact the Fernkloof Nature Reserve Office:\nTel:      
 028-3124148\nEmail:botsochermanus@telkomsa.net\nWebsite:   www.fernkloof.co
 m
